## Document 001: 10 月の金融政策決定会合は利上げ見送りへ 政策金利の２％超えは？

Source PDF: all report pdf/10 月の金融政策決定会合は利上げ見送りへ 政策金利の２％超えは？.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 14 日(水)

1/3 
10 月の金融政策決定会合は利上げ見送りへ  
政策金利の２％超えは？ 
 経済調査部主席エコノミスト藤代宏一 
（℡：050-5474-6123）

・10月30日の金融政策決定会合に先立って、早くも観測記事が金融市場を賑わせている。米系
通信は「日銀は今月会合で利上げ見送る公算大、12月の可能性排除せず」と報じた。記事内
では「経済・物価は日銀の見通しに沿って推移しており、２会合連続での利上げが必要なほ
ど物価の上振れリスクが一段と強まっている情勢にはない」、とはいえ「一時的な要因を除
いた基調的な物価上昇率は上昇を続けており、目標の２％から上振れていくリスクには依然
として注意が必要だ」などとする日銀関係者の発言が記述されていた。また既報のとおり、
基調的物価上昇率が概ね２％に達したとの判断を展望レポートに記すことにも触れられてい
た。

・10月の利上げ見送りの根拠として、昨日発表された９月の企業物価指数は一定の存在感があ
る。国内企業物価指数は前月比▲0.3％、前年比＋6.8％と上昇が一服し、市場予想の同＋
7.6％も下回った。原油価格が高止まりするなか、世界的なＡＩ・半導体関連需要の拡大など
を背景に非鉄金属や化学製品などで上昇が続いた一方、米を中心とする農林水産物の価格下
落が下押しに寄与した。円ベースの輸入物価指数は前年比＋24.5％と大幅な上昇を記録した
・日経平均株価は先行き12 ヶ月72,000 円程度で推移するだろう 
・USD/JPY は先行き12 ヶ月 155 円程度で推移するだろう 
・日銀は政策金利を12 月に 1.5％とした後、27 年末までに2.25％とするだろう 
・FED はFF 金利を、年内は4.0％で据え置くだろう

### Page 2

2/3 
が、８月の同＋25.1％からは小幅に鈍化した。川上段階のインフレ圧力は依然として強いも
のの、９月会合時点の判断に大きな修正を迫るものではなかったと考えられる。

・同日発表された「生活意識に関するアンケート調査」（９月調査）も似たような結果であっ
た。５年後の物価予想については「かなり上がる」の割合が51.1％と前回調査対比で概ね横
ばいとなり、「少し上がる」との合計では86.5％と高止まりした。他方、今後５年間の物価
上昇率（年平均）は、中央値が５％で横ばいも、平均値は＋10.1％と前回調査対比で0.7％pt
低下した。ガソリン代の補助に加え、米価の下落が効いたとみられ、家計の予想物価上昇率
が一段と上放れする事態は回避された。政府による家計支援策が効いているとはいえ、家計
の予想物価上昇率が低下したことは、日銀に時間的余裕を与え得る。なお、景況感について
は「１年前との比較」と「１年後の予想」の双方とも大きな変化は認められなかった。

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
・先行きの金融政策はどうなるであろうか。筆者は、10月会合は利上げ見送り、12月会合で利
上げを決定し、政策金利は1.5％で越年すると予想する。Ｆｅｄの利上げ観測が燻り、円安圧
力が強いなか、日銀が金融引き締め方向への手を緩めるとなれば、一段と円安が進行しかね
ない。また日銀短観（９月調査）を見る限り、企業の金融環境に大きな変化はみられず、既
往の利上げが実体経済に染み渡っている様子はそれほど窺えない。これらを踏まえ、筆者は
2027年も３～４回の利上げがあり、政策金利は２％を超えることを想定している。

・ただし、Ｆｅｄが2027年に政策金利を据え置き、その次の政策変更が利下げになるとの見方
が生じれば、日銀は円安対策から解放され、利上げを中断ないしは終了する可能性がある。
その点、ウィリアムズ・ＮＹ連銀総裁とウォラー理事の発言は注視しておきたい。まずウィ
リアムズ総裁は10月13日に「来年はインフレがかなり鈍化し、通年でみれば、全体のインフ
レ率は目標⁠の２％近辺になるとみている。これは私が言及した要因、すなわち関税の影響が
完全に過去のものとなることを反映している」として、2027年に利上げは必要ないとの見方
を示した。

・⁠ウォラー理事は10月13日に「最近の米国債利回りの上昇は経済成長の加速とＡＩへの大規模
投資によるもので、インフレ期待の高まりが原因ではない」、「インフレ期待は引き続き
『しっかりと安定している』」と述べ、その上で「データセンター建設を含むＡＩ投資が経
済の需要を支えており、今後２～３年はその効果が続く⁠可能性がある。大幅な生産性向上が
実現するのは、なお『数年先』だ」とした。同理事の発言を整理すると、①予想インフレは
安定しており、その点において現在の金融政策は適切である、②ＡＩ投資が一巡すれば、そ
れに起因するインフレ圧力は鈍化する、③その後に生産性向上によるディスインフレ方向へ
の動きが予想される、といったところであろう。

・両氏の予想が現実のものになれば、Ｆｅｄの利上げ観測は後退し、場合によっては2027年に
利下げ観測が台頭することもあり得る。そうした環境下、日銀は利上げペースを緩める、或
いは終了を検討するなど、多くの選択肢を手にすることができる。

## Document 013: 158円を挟んで膠着するドル円相場 ～中間選挙・イラン・金融政策の_三つの分岐点_が方向性を決める～

Source PDF: all report pdf/158円を挟んで膠着するドル円相場 ～中間選挙・イラン・金融政策の_三つの分岐点_が方向性を決める～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Investment Navigator

2026 年 10 月 22 日

1/6 
158 円を挟んで膠着するドル円相場 
～中間選挙・イラン・金融政策の"三つの分岐点"が方向性を決める～ 
 経済調査部 シニア・フェロー  嶌峰 義清 
（℡：050-5474-7489）

（要旨） 
○ ドル円相場は、7 月末の日米協調介入、9 月上旬の急激な円高、9 月の日米同時利上げを経て、
10 月に入ってからは1 ドル＝158 円前後の狭いレンジでの推移が続いている。円先物市場に
おける非商業部門の建玉は、9 月前半に過去に例のない規模で円の売り越しから買い越しへ
と転じた。しかし、その後は買い越し幅が縮小し、売建玉が再び増え始めている。投機筋の
円買い意欲は弱まりつつあると言えよう。 
○ 期待インフレ率 （BEI） で実質化した日米の実質金利差は、政策金利・2 年債・10 年債のいず
れでも 2％ポイントを超える米国優位が続いている。名目金利差は縮小したものの、米国の
金利上昇は実質金利の上昇、日本のは期待インフレ率の上昇によるところが大きい。このた
め、実質ベースのドル優位は崩れていない。 
○ 当面の焦点は、①11 月 3 日の米中間選挙、②選挙後のイラン情勢、③日銀の 12 月利上げと
その後のペース、の三つである。民主党が上下両院を制し、イラン情勢が沈静化に向かい、
日銀が利上げ路線を明確にすれば、円高方向への転換が視野に入る。逆の組み合わせとなれ
ば、再び160 円台を試し、介入への警戒が強まる展開も否定できない。

1. 投機筋の円ポジションは"円売り一色"から変化し、158 円を挟んで膠着が続くドル円相場 
ドル円相場は、方向感を欠いた推移が続いている。10 月入り後の値動きは、高値が158.94 円
（10 月14 日） 、安値が156.99 円（10 月2 日）と、月間の値幅は2 円弱にとどまる。 
もっとも、ここに至るまでの道のりは平坦ではなかった。4 月末から5 月の大型連休中には、
160 円台後半まで進んだ円安に対して、政府・日銀が総額11.7 兆円の円買い介入を実施した。そ
れでも円安基調は止まらず、7 月 28 日には163.91 円（ECB参照値）と、約40 年ぶりの円安水準を
つけた。これに対し、7 月 31 日には日米当局が28 年ぶりとなる円買い協調介入に踏み切った。介
入額は 15 兆3993 億円に達し、年初からの累計は27 兆 1342 億円と暦年で過去最大を更新した。 
その後は円高へと自律的な反発も見られた。9 月2～3 日には、日銀の追加利上げ観測の強まり
や米雇用関連指標の下振れを受けてドル円は1 日で3 円以上下落、9 日には153 円台まで円高が進
んだ。この局面では介入実績は無く、ジャクソンホール会議でのウォーシュFRB議長の発言をきっ

### Page 2

2/6 
かけに積み上がったドル買い・円売りの持ち高が、損切りを迫られた結果と考えられる。 
ところが、その後は再び円安に押し戻された。FRBは 9 月 16 日に約3 年ぶりの利上げに踏み切
り、政策金利を3.75～4.00％とした。日銀も18 日に政策金利を1.25％へ引き上げたものの、日
米がそろって利上げしたことで金利差は大きく変わらず、ドル円は158 円台へと戻っている。 
為替市場における"投機的な動き"の指標とされるのが、円先物市場における非商業部門（主に
ヘッジファンドなどとされる）の建玉である。その推移を見ると、ここ数ヶ月で大きな変化が生
じていることがわかる（図表1）。 
6 月 2 日時点の円売建玉は24 万 4416 枚と、円キャリートレードがブームだった2007 年以来の
高水準に達していた。差引（ネット）でも12 万9567 枚の売り越しだった。その後も、9 月1 日時
点では 9 万2227 枚の売り越しと、円売り超の状態が続いていた。 
しかし、9 月前半の急激な円高を境に状況は一変し、9 月 15 日時点では差引が12 万 0359 枚の
買い越しへと転じ、わずか2 週間で円買い方向に21 万枚強も振れた。この振れ幅は、統計をさか
のぼれる1986 年以降で最大である。同時点の円買建玉（23 万 7951 枚）も過去最大を記録した。 
もっとも、その後は買い越し幅の縮小が続いている。10 月13 日時点では、買建玉19 万 1256 枚
に対し売建玉14 万 1049 枚で、差引の買い越しは5 万0207 枚とピークの半分以下に縮んだ。売建
玉は 9 月末の12 万枚台から14 万枚台へと再び増えており、円売りの持ち高が積み上がり始めて
いる兆しもうかがえる。投資主体別に見ると、レバレッジド・ファンドは10 月 13 日時点で3 万
3673 枚の円売り越しとなっており、ヘッジファンドの円買い意欲がすでに後退していることを示
している。 
足元の売建玉は、過去の介入局面のように当局が介入を急がなければならない水準にはない。
一方で、9 月に積み上がった円の買い持ちの解消そのものが、円安圧力として作用している面もあ
ると考えられる。

3/6
2.  実質金利差から見たドル円相場
為替相場の方向性を最終的に決めるのは、日米の金融政策と、それを反映した金利差である。
そこで、日米の実質金利差を、政策金利・2 年債・10 年債の3 つの年限で見てみる（図表2） 。こ
こでは、実質化に用いる物価として、市場が織り込む期待インフレ率であるBEI（ブレーク・イー
ブン・インフレ率、10 年債）を用いた。足元の消費者物価は、米国では原油高で押し上げられ、
日本では電気・ガス料金支援で押し下げられており、実勢を見誤りやすいためである。
名目で見ると、政策金利は米FF金利の誘導目標が3.75～4.00％、日本の無担保コール翌日物が
1.25％である。10 月 21 日時点の国債利回りは、米国が2 年債4.79％・10 年債 5.13％、日本が2
年債 1.886％・10 年債2.882％となっている。一方、10 年BEIは米国が2.35％（10 月20 日） 、日
本が 2.1～2.2％程度と、ほぼ同水準である。これを差し引いて実質化すると、筆者の試算ではお

おむね次のようになる。
✓ 政策金利：米国＋1.5～1.6％程度、日本▲0.9～1.0％程度（米国優位・差は約2.5％ポイント）
✓ 2 年債：米国＋2.4％程度、日本▲0.3％程度（米国優位・差は約2.7％ポイント）
✓ 10 年債：米国＋2.8％程度、日本＋0.7％程度（米国優位・差は約2.1％ポイント）
名目で見れば、日本の10 年債利回りが9 月下旬に3.115％と約 30 年ぶりの水準に達するなど、
日米金利差は縮小してきた。しかし、BEIで実質化すると景色は異なる。すべての年限で米国の実
質金利が2％ポイント以上も上回っており、ドル優位の構図はほとんど崩れていないのである。
その背景には、日米で金利上昇の"中身"が異なることがある。米国では、昨年末から長期金利
が約 1％ポイント上昇したが、BEIは 2.25％から2.35％へとほぼ横ばいにとどまった。名目金利の
注：BEIで実質化

### Page 4

4/6 
上昇は、ほぼそのまま実質金利の上昇となり、10 年物の実質利回りは9 月末に 2.93％と2008 年
以来の高水準に達した。これに対し日本では、BEIが過去最高圏の2％台まで上昇しており、名目
金利の上昇の相応の部分を期待インフレ率の上昇が占めている。実際、9 月の日銀の利上げ後も
BEIはむしろ上昇した。日本の金利上昇は"インフレ期待による金利上昇"の色彩が濃く、実質金利
の上昇は限られていると言えよう。 
日本の政策金利は、実質では依然として▲1％近いマイナス圏にあり、金融環境はなお緩和的
だ。日銀の利上げや介入にもかかわらず円安の流れが変わらなかったのは、こうした実質金利差
の構図で説明できる面が大きい。逆に言えば、日本の期待インフレ率が2％近辺で落ち着いたまま
利上げが進むか、米国の実質金利が低下に転じない限り、本格的な円高への転換は難しいと考え
られる。 
なお、日本の物価連動国債は発行残高が約10 兆円と小さく、BEIには流動性の影響も含まれ
る。政策金利や2 年債を10 年BEIで実質化するのも簡便的な手法である点には留意が必要だ。

3.  中間選挙の結果次第で変わるドルの行方 
11 月3 日には米国の中間選挙が行われる。1934 年以降の23 回の中間選挙のうち、現職大統領
の政党が上下両院とも議席を増やしたのはわずか2 回しかない。今回も、ロイター通信の世論調
査ではトランプ大統領の支持率が過去最低の31％まで落ち込み、投票先では民主党47％、共和党
35％と 12 ポイントの差がついている。選挙予測サイトのディシジョン・デスクHQは、民主党が下
院を制する確率を78％、上院を制する確率を56％と見ている。 
結果ごとに、ドル円相場への影響を整理すると次のようになる。 
① 民主党が上下両院を制する場合：予算や減税、関税政策に対する議会のチェックが強まり、
財政拡張への期待が後退することで、米長期金利の低下とドル安につながりやすい。FRBの人事に
対する議会の影響力が強まり、金融政策の独立性への懸念が和らぐことも、長期金利の押し下げ
要因となろう。一方で、予算案を巡る対立が先鋭化すれば、政府機関の閉鎖などを通じたリスク
回避の円買いが生じる可能性もある。いずれにしても、ドル円には下押し圧力がかかる公算が大
きい。 
② 下院は民主党、上院は共和党の"ねじれ"となる場合：現在の政策の大枠が維持される一方
で、新たな大型財政出動は難しくなる。相場の反応は限定的で、関心は再び日米の金融政策へと
移ることになろう。 
③ 共和党が両院の過半数を維持する場合：減税や規制緩和など拡張的な政策への期待から、米
長期金利の上昇とドル高が進みやすい。ドル円が再び160 円台を試し、介入への警戒が一気に高
まる展開も想定される。 
世論調査どおりであれば①ないし②の公算が大きく、ドル円にはどちらかと言えば円高寄りの
材料となろう。ただし、重要なのは選挙後の財政・通商政策や金利見通しの変化であり、選挙直
後の値動きだけで方向性を判断するのは早計だろう。

### Page 5

5/6 
4.  イラン情勢：選挙後の"重し"が外れる 
もう一つの大きな不透明要因がイラン情勢である。2 月 28 日に始まった米国・イスラエルとイ
ランの戦闘は、ホルムズ海峡の事実上の封鎖と、米国によるイラン港湾の海上封鎖を伴ったまま
長期化している。北海ブレント原油先物は20 日に一時1 バレル＝111.99 ドルと約5 ヶ月ぶりの高
値をつけた。 
原油高は、いくつかの経路でドル高円安に作用している。米国では、ガソリン価格の前年比
34％の上昇などを背景に、9 月の消費者物価が同＋3.7％へと加速し、FRBの追加利上げ観測を支え
ている。日本にとっては、エネルギー輸入額の増加を通じて貿易収支が悪化し、実需の円売りが
増える。加えて、地政学リスクの高まりは"有事のドル買い"も誘いやすい。 
注目されるのは、トランプ大統領が「中間選挙前にはイランを攻撃しない」と明言している点
だ。言い換えれば、選挙後はこの制約が外れることになる。選挙後に軍事行動が再開されれば、
原油価格の一段高と米利上げ観測の強まりを通じて、ドル高円安が進むリスクがある。逆に、仲
介国を通じた交渉がまとまり、海峡の再開に向かえば、原油価格の下落とともに米長期金利が低
下し、ドル安円高へと振れやすい。実際、20～21 日には原油安を受けて米長期金利が低下し、ド
ルが売られる場面が見られた。

5.  日米の金融政策：12 月の"同時利上げ"の先が焦点 
FRBは9 月に約3 年ぶりの利上げに踏み切り、年内にもう1 回の利上げを示唆した。10 月14 日
に9 月の消費者物価が前年比3.7％と予想を上回ると、27～28 日のFOMCでの利上げ確率は一時5
割前後まで上昇した。しかし、17 日にウォーシュ議長が10 月会合の判断を予断しない姿勢を示し
たことで、利上げ確率は1 割台半ばまで低下している。市場では「10 月は見送り、12 月に利上
げ」との見方が大勢となった。一部には、12 月が今回の利上げ局面で最後になるとの予想も出て
いる。 
一方、日銀の植田総裁は、9 月の利上げ後に「政策の局面は変化した」と述べ、その後も基調的
な物価上昇率が2％に「かなり近づいている」との認識を示した。そのうえで、 「これ以上上がる
ことは容認できない」とも踏み込んでいる。29～30 日の決定会合では政策金利が据え置かれる公
算が大きいが、基調物価が2％に到達したと判断されると報じられている。 
日米がともに12 月に利上げすれば、名目の政策金利差は変わらない。為替にとって重要なの
は、その先の方向性の違いである。FF金利が明確に中立水準を超えるFRBの利上げが最終局面に近
づく一方、日銀は中立金利を目指して利上げを続ける。この構図が鮮明になれば、日米の実質金
利差は徐々に縮小に向かおう。逆に、原油高を受けて米国の物価上昇が加速し、FRBが一段の利上
げを迫られる展開となれば、実質金利差の縮小は見込めず、ドル円相場の膠着状態を演出しよ
う。

6.  今後の見通し：年末に向けて緩やかな円高を想定、ただし分岐は大きい 
以上を踏まえると、当面のドル円相場は、日米の金融政策会合と中間選挙という大きなイベン

### Page 6

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
6/6 
トを控え、当面は現状の狭いレンジでの推移が続くと考えられる。10 月27～28 日のFOMCは据え置
きが市場の大勢だ。FOMCで利上げが見送られ、その後の日銀会合で基調物価2％到達が確認されて
12 月利上げへの地ならしが進めば、円高方向に振れやすい組み合わせとなる。しかし、市場では
FRBも年内利上げを実施するとの見方が強く、ドル円相場を動かす材料とはなりにくい。逆に、
FRBが10 月のFOMCで利上げを決定した場合、ドル円相場は159 円を超える円安進展の可能性が高
まる。 
年末にかけては、①中間選挙で民主党が議会の主導権を握り、②イラン情勢が交渉による解決
に向かい原油価格が落ち着き、③日銀が12 月に利上げを実施して来年以降も利上げ継続の姿勢を
示す、という三つの条件がそろえば、日米の実質金利差は縮小に向かう。その場合は、150 円台半
ばから前半への円高が進む可能性がある。投機筋の売建玉が再び積み上がっている局面であれ
ば、その巻き戻しが円高の勢いを増幅することも考えられる。 
一方で、①共和党の善戦、②選挙後のイランへの軍事行動の再開、③FRBの利上げ継続＋日銀の
利上げ慎重姿勢打ち出し、といった組み合わせとなれば、ドル円は再び160 円台を試すことにな
ろう。その場合、政府・日銀は過去の介入水準である160 円台前半から164 円近辺を意識しつ
つ、円安のスピードを抑えるための介入に踏み切る公算が大きい。ただし、これまでの経験が示
すように、ファンダメンタルズが変わらない中での介入は"時間を買う"政策にすぎず、効果が一
時的にとどまる可能性には注意が必要だ。 
筆者は、①米中間選挙は最低でも上下院のねじれ、場合によっては上院においても民主党の過
半数獲得、②選挙後に米国がイランへの軍事行動を再開、同時に仲介国を介した交渉を再開する
ものの、膠着状態が続く、③日米とも利上げ継続、をメインシナリオとしている。 
さらにリスク要因として、①米中間選挙の結果がなかなか確定せず、議会勢力の確定に時間が
かかる、②イラン情勢はフーシ派から攻撃を受けているサウジアラビアや、総選挙を終えたイス
ラエルによる本格的な攻撃参戦、の可能性を挙げる。 
このように不透明要因が多いことも足元のドル円相場の膠着の要因になっていると考えられる
が、短期的な値動きに一喜一憂するよりも、実質金利差という基本的な決定要因がどちらに動い
ているかを冷静に見極めることが重要であり、日米中央銀行の金融政策姿勢が最も注目される。

## Document 142: 261020_ml_nienmade

Source PDF: all report pdf/261020_ml_nienmade.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-20

### Page 1

Nien Made Enterprise

3Q preview: EPS +13% YoY to NT$6.6; 
Better 2H but not yet a turnaround

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 441.00 TWD | Price: 358.50 TWD

3Qe EPS NT$6.6: strong rev and GP offset by opex and FX 
Nien Made is scheduled to report 3Q26 results on 4 Nov. Disclosed monthly sales imply 
3Q26 revenue growth of 16% YoY to NT$8.5bn, or 9% in USD terms, largely 
volume-driven. Growth reaccelerated from 1.7% YoY in 1H26, led by custom-made on a 
low US base and expansion in Europe and Japan. Ready-made also improved as 
Cambodia labor shortages eased. (See Exhibit 1 for US window coverings spending 
trends). Gross margin: We expect underlying GPM to rise 1.1ppt YoY to 59.8% on lower 
tariffs, broadly flat QoQ. We forecasts an NT$300mn of IEEPA tariff refunds in 2H26. 
Assuming NT$150mn is booked in 3Q, this would add 1.7ppt and lift reported GPM to 
61.5%. We expect the selling expense ratio to fall 1.7ppt QoQ to 17.5% on lower fuel 
charges, but remain 0.5ppt higher YoY due to continued salesforce and distribution 
network investment. We expect an FX loss in 3Q26 vs. an NT$118mn gain in 3Q25, 
weighing on YoY earnings growth. We forecast 3Q26 EPS of NT$6.6, up 13% YoY. 
US window covering picking up, but no turnaround yet  
BofA credit/debit card data suggests US spending on window coverings turned positive 
in Aug-26 for the first time after 19 consecutive months of YoY declines. Spending rose 
4%/3% YoY in Aug/Sep, respectively. This improvement partly explains the recent 
reacceleration in Nien Made's monthly sales. That said, we view the improvement more 
as normalization off a low base than the start of an industry turnaround. The Aug-Sep 
2025 comparison base was relatively weak, while the current rate-hike environment 
remains a headwind to housing-related demand. 
Slightly raise FY26 EPS est. to NT$22.9; PO to NT$441 
We raise our FY26 EPS estimate by 2% to NT$22.9 to reflect the blended impact of 
stronger-than-expected revenue growth and FX loss. We maintain our FY26 DPS forecast 
of NT$17 per share. We raise our PO by 4% to NT$441, as we roll forward our valuation 
basis from FY26E EPS to FY27E EPS. At the same time, we lower our target P/E from 
19x to 17x, reflecting the MSCI removal and a less supportive rate environment.

Company Sector 
Interior Furnishings

Company Description 
Nien Made, founded in 1974, is one of the world's largest 
window-covering manufacturers. It has roughly 50% 
market share in both ready-made (OEM business) shades 
and blinds, and custom-made (own brand) shutters in the 
US. The US market accounted for over 75% of sales. Key 
clients include Home Depot and Wal-Mart for ready-made 
shades and blinds. Nien Made operates its own retail 
network and e-commerce business for its custom-made 
shutters under the Norman brand name in the US and 
Europe.

Investment Rationale 
We have Buy rating on Nien Made, mainly given 1) clear 
custom-made growth profile (share gain in the US and fast-
growing DTC in Japan and Europe), 2) its largest customer 
Home Depot is gaining market share, and 3) margin 
expansion potentials backed by favorable products mix.

Price objective basis & risk 
Nien Made Enterprise (XNRBF) 
Our PO NT$441 is derived from 17x P/E 2027E EPS. This is around 0.5SD above 
historical the one-year forward P/E, which we think is warranted given the visibility of 
the growth profile.

Downside risks are 1) weaker-than-expected Home Depot's sell-through, 2) slow 
recovery of US housing turnover, and 3) higher tariff increases on China exports to the 
US. 4) Loss of key customers.

大立光短期營運受智慧型手機新機拉貨動能趨緩影響 ， 加上新產
品良率及綠電採購成本壓力 ， 預期第四季營收及獲利表現仍相對
保守。展望 2027 年，手機主鏡頭持續朝高規格升級，有利公司
發揮精密光學設計與製造優勢；另一方面， FAU 預計於 2H27
進入量產，隨 CPO 技術逐步發展，有望成為中長期新增營收來
源。 考量大立光具備高精度光學製造、 微米級對位技術及自動化
設備開發能力， 技術門檻高且具備跨領域應用優勢， 正面看待公
司中長期發展。綜合以上，研究部預估大立光 2027 年營業收入
為 706.92 億元，Y+17.08%，毛利率 51.37%，Y+4.53ppts，
營業利益率 40.04%，Y+4.98ppts，稅後純益 268.24 億元，
Y+24.96%，EPS 為 205.08 元。研究部考量儘管 2026 年消費
性電子需求疲弱，且 CPO 產品短期營收貢獻仍有限，然而進度
優於先前預期，隨新產品逐步放量，2027 年營運有望重回成長
軌道，給予大立光 38 倍本益比評價，目標價訂為 7,800 元，投
資建議為買進。  
⚫ 公司基本資料 
⚫ Largan 大立光電(3008.TT)公司簡介 
大立光電(3008.TT)成立於 1987 年，並於 2002 年台灣證券交易所
掛牌上市。大立光作為全球光學塑膠鏡頭領導廠商，主要業務內容
為研發與製造精密光學塑膠鏡頭，產品主要應用於智慧型手機，其
餘也包含平板、筆記型電腦、車用及其他消費性電子產品，在全球
光學鏡頭市占率約佔 30%。其總部位於台中市南屯區精密機械園
區，共有十座廠區及兩座興建中廠區。前十大股東持股合計
38.06%。客戶為 APPLE、華為、三星、小米、OPPO、VIVO 等手
機品牌廠，主要競爭者則為玉晶光 (3406.TT) 及舜宇光學
(2382.HK)。3Q26 出貨量佔比：20MP 以上約 10~20%、10MP 約

⚫ 產業概況 
記憶體成本飆升壓抑出貨，智慧型手機市場進入量縮價增 
根據研調機構數據 ，2026 年全球智慧型手機市場受記憶體供應短缺
及成本大幅上漲影響 ， 出貨量預估將Y-16.7%至 10.5 億支 ， 較前一
季預估的 Y-13.9%再度下修，並創下歷年最大年度衰退幅度，其中
2H26 出貨量更預估 Y-27.2%。 由於記憶體成本年增超過300%，品
牌廠難以自行吸收成本，陸續透過漲價及調整產品組合因應，帶動
2026 年智慧型手機平均售價 Y+27.6%至 581 美元，全球市場產值
反而 Y+6.3%至 6,130 億美元，呈現「量縮價增」格局。產品結構
方面，低階市場受到衝擊最為明顯，尤其100 美元以下機種因原先
毛利空間有限，品牌廠陸續縮減產品線，2Q26 該價位出貨量已年
減近 60%；相較之下，高階機種因消費者價格敏感度較低，且成熟
市場具分期付款等機制支撐，需求相對具韌性，帶動整體市場加速
朝高階化發展。 
展望未來，記憶體供需緊張預期至少延續至 2028 年，全球市場短

展望 4Q26，預期 10 月拉貨量與 9 月持平，11 月起則隨新機拉貨
高峰結束而逐步下滑 ， 儘管客戶1H27 新產品已自 10 月開始拉貨，

短，第四季營收動能仍具不確定性。毛利率方面，儘管新機生產良
率逐步改善 ， 然而綠電採購成本持續增加 ， 相關成本最快須至2028

Y-18.28%，EPS 為 41.98 元。綜合以上，研究部預估大立光 2026

主。獲利方面，因綠電採購成本持續增加，短期毛利率改善仍將取
決於新產品良率及產品組合變化。

光通方面，FA 產品已通過客戶認證，FAU 則持續進行送樣及整合
驗證 ，FAU 預計 2027 年年中完成產線建置 ，2H27 正式進入量產。
初期以單排 FA 及 Grating Couplin 技術為主，並朝 FAU 與 Carrier
整合出貨發展 ， 提高單一產品附加價值 ， 多排FA 及 Edge Coupling

自製自動化設備及檢測能力 ， 可望在高技術門檻的FAU 市場建立競
爭優勢。預期 2027 年光通營收貢獻仍處於起步階段，隨後續產能
與良率提升，有望成為中長期成長動能。綜合以上，研究部預估大

⚫ 結論與建議 
大立光短期營運受智慧型手機新機拉貨動能趨緩影響，加上新產品
良率及綠電採購成本壓力，預期第四季營收及獲利表現仍相對保
守。展望 2027 年，手機主鏡頭持續朝高規格升級，有利公司發揮
精密光學設計與製造優勢 ； 另一方面 ，FAU 預計於 2H27 進入量產 ，
隨 CPO 技術逐步發展，有望成為中長期新增營收來源。考量大立
光具備高精度光學製造、微米級對位技術及自動化設備開發能力，
技術門檻高且具備跨領域應用優勢，正面看待公司中長期發展。綜
合以上，研究部預估大立光 2027 年營業收入為 706.92 億元，
Y+17.08%，毛利率 51.37%，Y+4.53ppts，營業利益率 40.04%，
Y+4.98ppts，稅後純益 268.24 億元，Y+24.96%，EPS 為 205.08
元。研究部考量儘管2026 年消費性電子需求疲弱 ，且CPO 產品短
期營收貢獻仍有限，然而進度優於先前預期，隨新產品逐步放量，
2027 年營運有望重回成長軌道，給予大立光 38 倍本益比評價，目
標價訂為 7,800 元，投資建議為買進。

## Document 175: 6467泰合 永豐

Source PDF: all report pdf/6467泰合 永豐.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

個股聚焦  
2026/10/20

陳奕均 fion.chen@sinopac.com

泰合 (6467 TT) 
開花結果在即

永豐觀點 
抗血栓口溶膜貼片 BEQIA 歐美取證在即。 
 投資評價與建議 
投資建議買進：泰合 10/20 上市掛牌，掛牌價105.6 元。由於(1) 泰合開發之口溶
膜劑型藥物有效解決高齡與吞嚥困難之臨床痛點下，市場競爭力足；(2) 抗血栓口
溶膜貼片 BEQIA 歐美取證在即，2027 年在授權與上市權利金開始認列下 ，營運轉
虧為盈且呈大跳躍狀；(3) 由於多數學名藥廠受限專利訴訟，最快需至 2028 年 4
月後才可上市銷售，促使泰合享有約一年的「黃金市場空窗期」 ，能與原廠直接瓜
分市場。且因 BEQIA 屬 505(b)(2)新劑型新藥，即便2Q28 後學名藥陸續進場，其
獨特的劑型優勢仍能築起價格防護牆，所面臨的價折壓力相對輕微，建議買進，目
標價 170 元( 18 X 2027 EPS )。

營運現況與分析 
公司簡介：泰合生技成立於 2010 年，專注開發 505(b)(2)新劑型新藥與特殊學名
藥，核心技術為自主研發的 TDS 透皮／透黏膜傳輸平台，將現有口服藥品成分改
造為透皮與透黏膜藥物傳導劑型 ，提供方便使用且更安全的新劑型藥物。 產品開發
上，505(b)(2)部分，治療抗血栓口溶膜貼片(每日兩片)BEQIA 靜待歐美取證中外，
其餘則仍在臨床階段中，困難學名藥部分，化療止吐口溶膜2018 年日本取證銷售
中外，其餘四項藥品則在進行洽談授權合作。 
圖一：泰合產品線開發時程

資料來源：泰合；永豐投顧研究部整理，Oct. 2026

### Page 2

2

口溶膜劑型藥物能有效解決高齡與吞嚥困難之臨床痛點 ：傳統口服錠劑藥物必須經
過食道、進入胃腸道消化，再經由肝臟代謝後才進入血液循環，泰合開發之口溶膜
劑型藥物， 可讓藥膜在接觸舌頭或口腔黏膜數秒內快速溶解 ， 病患完全不需飲水即
可完成給藥， 且部份藥物分子能直接透過口腔黏膜上皮細胞微血管吸收， 直接進入
全身血液循環， 不僅能提高藥物生體可用率， 還能減少胃酸對藥物的破壞與對胃腸
道的刺激下，有效解決高齡與吞嚥困難之臨床痛點，提高患者服藥順從性。 
抗血栓口溶膜貼片 BEQIA 歐美取證在即，提前卡位下，營運動能可期：泰合自主
開發的抗血栓口溶膜新劑型新藥 BEQIA， 預計4Q26 取得歐盟藥證 、2Q27 獲美國
FDA 核准上市。目前該藥原廠在美國市場年銷售額>200 億美元，相較原廠口服錠
劑，泰合 BEQIA 具備三大競爭優勢：不需飲水（服藥便利）、解決吞嚥困難（減
輕照顧端壓力），以及攜帶與給藥便利（優化長照與醫療機構給藥流程）。 
市場競爭方面，由於多數學名藥廠受限專利訴訟，最快需至2028 年 4 月後才可上
市銷售，促使泰合享有約一年的「黃金市場空窗期」 ，能與原廠直接瓜分市場。此
外，由於BEQIA 屬 505(b)(2)新劑型新藥，即便2Q28 後學名藥陸續進場，其獨特
的劑型優勢仍能築起價格防護牆，所面臨的折價壓力應相對輕微。 
泰合海外市場營銷預計將採授權分潤模式，研究部預估 1H27 歐美地區簽約金應
有機會各達 500~1000 萬美金外，隨美國地區 2Q27 上市、通路鋪設及醫保陸續
准入，Peak sales 市佔率 2-3%(預期將落於 2029-30 年)、銷售分潤 12-15%估算
下，稅後 EPS 貢獻將落於 10-20 元。 
財務預估：預估泰合 2026 年營收 0.1 億元，稅後虧損 1.89 億元，稅後 EPS 以掛
牌後股本 5.99 億元估算，稅後 EPS-3.15 元，2027 年預估歐美地區授權簽約金各
500 萬美金及 1000 萬美金 ， 及上市銷售後權利金認列下 ，預估營收將跳升至7.75
億元，稅後淨利 5.68 億元，稅後 EPS 9.49 元。

## Document 195: [000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 소음보다 방향성에SK하이닉스(000660) 소음보다 방향성에소음보다 방향성에 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 소음보다 방향성에SK하이닉스(000660) 소음보다 방향성에소음보다 방향성에 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-09-28
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

기업분석 2025.09.28 www.daishin.com

Issue & News 
SK하이닉스  
(000660) 
 소음보다 방향성에 
류형근

hyungkeun.ryu@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 목표주가 3,200,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.09.23) 
1,862,000 
반도체업종

◼ 최근 시장은 2가지 Risk를 제기. 다만, 과도한 우려는 기우. 
◼ 현 주가는 보다 강인해지는 Fundamental의 가치를 미반영한 수준. 
◼ 비중 확대 전략 유효. 목표주가와 매수의견 유지.

투자전략: 업황을 대변하지 못하고 있는 주가 
목표주가와 매수의견 유지. 현 주가는 업황의 강세를 충분히 반영하지 못하
고 있다고 생각. 1) 메모리반도체 사이클의 추가 확장 (구매 경쟁 심화에 따른 
단기 가격 상승 폭 확대, 기대치를 상회할 4Q26 DRAM 판매량), 2) 3Q26 실
적발표에서 보다 구체화될 주주환원 정책의 방향성에 보다 집중할 필요. 
시장에서 제기하는 Risk가 있다면, 2가지. 1) 주요 고객사향 HBM4 품질 이슈, 
2) Solidigm IPO와 해외 공장 투자에 따른 경계심리 확대가 그 대상. 당사는 
해당 Risk가 Fundamental을 저해할 것으로 보고 있지 않으며, 단기 노이즈보
다는 사이클 확장의 선순환에 보다 주목할 필요가 있다고 생각. 
1. HBM4 품질 이슈, 과도한 우려는 기우 
3Q26부터 HBM4 출하가 본격화될 것이라는 기존 입장을 유지. HBM4 비중
은 3Q26 전체 판매량의 40%, 2027년 연간 50% 중반까지 상승할 것. 
일각에서는 주요 고객사향 HBM4 품질 이슈로 사업 성과가 기대치를 하회할 
수 있다는 우려를 표출. 다만, 과도한 우려는 기우. 후공정 단에서 문제가 일
부 발생했을 수 있으나, 해결 불가능한 Risk라 보기 어렵고, 불량에 따른 배
상금을 논할 단계도 아니라는 판단. 
2. 해외 공장 투자, 실보다 득이 크다 
언론보도에 따르면, SK하이닉스는 해외 공장 투자와 Solidigm IPO를 추진 중. 
해당 결정이 현실화될 경우, 시장은 단기적으로 이를 부정적으로 받아들일 
가능성이 존재. 다만, 결과적으로, 실보다 득이 큰 선택이 될 것이라 생각. 
- 해외 투자가 현실화되더라도, 실질 생산 기여 시점은 2028년 이후일 가능
성이 높음. 미국의 건설 경기와 공장 구축의 세부 단계 등을 감안할 필요. 
- 해외 투자 결정이 관세 Risk를 축소시킬 수 있는 변화라는 점도 감안할 필
요. “불확실성 해소”는 Upcycle의 중반부에서 반도체 주식에 대한 좋은 투자
포인트가 되어왔고, 이번에도 다르지 않을 것이라는 판단.

## Document 203: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

00

삼성전자 (005930/KS) 
현저한 저평가 
3Q26 영업이익 106 조원 예상 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익을 106조원 (+18% QoQ)으로 예상한다. 이는 기존 당사 
전망치 111 조원 대비 4.5% 하향된 것이다. 원/달러 환율 가정을 기존 1,450원에서 
1,400원으로 3.4% 하향, MX/NW 사업부의 적자 폭을 확대 반영 (-0.5조원 -> -
1.4조원)했기 때문이다. 주가의 핵심인 메모리 판가는 DRAM +17%, NAND +15%
로 오히려 예상을 소폭 상회할 것으로 예상된다. 출하는 수요 강세에도 불구하고 재고 
부족, HBM 확대에 따른 제한적 공급 여력으로 DRAM +4%, NAND +7%의 기존 예
상을 유지한다. HBM은 분기 최대 출하를 경신하기 시작할 것이다. 또한 AI 강세로 파
운드리 수주 및 가동률의 동반 상승이 지속 중이다. 성과 충당금 반영 등에 따라 단기 
흑자전환 가시성은 제한적이나, DS 사업부 전체적으로 강화되고 있는 국면이다.

2027 년 568 조원 (+55% YoY) 전망 유지. 환율 하락 반영 
SK증권은 삼성전자의 2027년 영업이익 전망치를 568조원으로 유지한다. 원/달러 환
율 가정을 기존 1,440원에서 1,340원으로 7% 하향했음에도, 실적 전망을 유지하는 
이유는 AI 메모리 수요 강세 속 공급 부족 심화, LTA에 따른 Dual market 효과에 의
한 Commodity 메모리 가격 강세, 2027년 HBM 판가 강세 (+103% YoY) 와 출하 
강세, 수익성 회복의 동반, 파운드리 적자 폭 축소의 본격화 전망 때문이다. 2027년 메
모리 출하는 DRAM +22%, NAND +16%, 판가는 DRAM +38%, NAND +28%
를 예상한다. 시장에서 우려하는 메모리 가격 하락, 감익 시나리오는 없을 것이다.

차별적 실적과 주주환원 본격화. 현저한 저평가 
SK증권은 메모리 업황과 삼성전자의 주가가 현저히 저평가 되어있다고 판단한다. 삼성
전자의 2027E P/E는 4.2X에 불과하며, 이익 창출력은 글로벌 최상위권이다. 주주환
원 정책 또한 기존의 FCF 50% 환원이 향후에도 유지될 것으로 가정한다면, 2027년 
193조원, 2028년 222조원으로 현재 시가총액 (우선주 포함) 대비 각각 11%, 13% 
수준에 육박한다. 성장을 위한 투자를 확대하고 있음에도, 대규모의 환원이 가능하다. 
LTA는 AI 시대 메모리 수요의 질과 실적 가시성을 제고시키며, 이는 주주환원의 지속
가능성을 의미한다. 메모리 가격의 높은 기저, LTA 물량의 계약 가격 적용을 감안하면, 
가격 상승률 자체는 안정화될 것이다. 다만 실적 안정성, 가시성, Duration을 확보하기 
시작했다는 점이 훨씬 중요한 가치이며, 아직 시장의 Playbook에 없을 뿐이다. 시대는 
변하고 있고, 시장도 변해야 한다. 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 610,000원을 유지한다. 
2026-10-06

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
As was universally expected, the RBA Monetary Policy
Board (MPB) lifted the cash rate by 25bps to 4.6% at its
September 2026 meeting.
This is quite the turnaround from the 11 August meeting,
where an extended period on hold looked to be the base case.

At that time, the data had broken against the RBA’s more
hawkish view, and its published forecasts did not support
further hikes. But the MPB can now point to the emergence of
some of the upside risks to inflation it warned of in August.
The post-meeting statement was notable for its emphasis
on the Middle East conflict, noting that “global energy prices
are now much higher than had been assumed in the August
forecasts” – “much higher” is stronger language than one

normally sees in an RBA statement. The RBA’s liaison indicates
that firms are passing this on or planning to do so. The pass-
through was characterised as partial, and additional to
domestically driven inflation arising from capacity pressures. We
read this as implying that the RBA is concerned about upside
risks to underlying inflation in Q4 of this year, as well as Q3.
The RBA is also wary about pressure on both construction
costs and retail prices of IT goods arising from the AI/data

centre boom, although in the media conference, the Governor
clarified that risks there were building rather than materialising.
The broader background highlighted in the Governor’s opening
statement to the media conference, is of demand outstripping
supply. The statement evidenced this by noting the stronger
July CPI and June quarter GDP results than expected.
The accompanying statement was notable for the absence of
language about the labour market being a little tight, despite

this being the inter-meeting message from the Governor
and other staff (see, for example, the Governor’s opening
statement on 18 September) and the language in the post-
meeting media conference. Views clearly differ on this: we
note that the Treasury forecasts underlying the May budget
and the recent Intergenerational Review imply both more
spare capacity in the labour market (a lower NAIRU) and faster
growth in potential from wherever the starting point is (higher

productivity growth and trend increase in participation).
Instead, the post-meeting statement characterised the
labour market as easing “broadly as expected”, despite the
unemployment rate already rising higher than the August SMP
forecast of 4.5% for the December quarter.
RBA hikes in September, November follow-up now expected
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The decision was unanimous, suggesting that any qualms
external MPB members might have had about the RBA’s

downbeat view of supply capacity were overruled by resurgent
oil prices and the stronger than forecast CPI and GDP data.
With upside risks being seen to have emerged, the post-meeting
statement language included that the Board “will continue to
do what it considers necessary to bring inflation sustainably
back to target, including increasing the cash rate target further if
needed”, a small but meaningful change from the “if upside risks
materialise” language from the previous meeting, and back to

the tone of the language at the June meeting.
The bar for a follow-up hike in November is low. Indeed,
judging by the post-meeting rhetoric, a November hike is now
the base case, absent a lasting resolution of the Middle East
conflict beforehand, or some other event that significantly
lowers the outlook for energy-related costs in Australia. The
bar for hikes beyond that is much higher, given the cumulative
rise in interest rates, and noting that the labour market is

easing and the housing market will likely weaken further.
The August inflation data released the day after the meeting
was in line with our expectations. We therefore continue to
expect Q3 trimmed mean inflation around 0.9%qtr, too high for
the RBA’s comfort, and upside risks for Q4. Likewise, there are
no implications for our rates view from this week’s inflation data.
Forecast changes
• We have updated our financial forecasts to reflect the change

in our rate view.
• Our labour market forecasts are unchanged on the first
decimal place, but there is some modest upside risk to our
unemployment forecast.
• Our updated GDP profile can be found on page 9.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: searching for signal
led the correction to date, the fact that the other capitals
are also reporting price declines clearly indicates that the
correction is deepening and broadening, as higher interest
rates collide with the tax policy changes announced in May’s

budget. Sentiment effects indeed remain at play, as evinced
by the continued pull-back in investor credit growth . Westpac
anticipates a peak-to-trough decline of around 7%, with tight
on-market supply limiting the extent of the correction. For
more, see our latest Housing Pulse.
Offshore, there was plenty of data to dissect.
In the US, inflation eased in August despite ongoing strength
in household demand and higher energy prices. Headline PCE
inflation rose 0.3%mth and 3.4%yr, while core PCE increased

0.2%mth and 3.0%yr. Encouragingly for the FOMC, the
share of PCE components rising by more than 3% over the
past year, a measure frequently cited by Fed Chairman Kevin
Warsh, declined to 45% from 49%, suggesting some easing in
broad-based inflation pressures. At the same time, household
spending remained robust, increasing 0.9%mth, equivalent
to around 0.6%mth in real terms. That said, personal income
growth was soft, rising 0.2% in the month, signalling

households opted to draw down savings.
The ISM manufacturing survey was stronger than the
headline suggested. While the index edged down 0.1pt to
54.5, manufacturing has now expanded for nine consecutive
months. There were some signs of capacity constraints
emerging, with production softened in the month but backlogs
up 4.6pts to 56.4, suggesting that firms struggled to keep
pace with demand. New orders rose 1.6pts to 55.3, primarily
due to domestic demand as new export orders eased, backlogs

increased. Firms responded by lifting employment, 1.5pts to
52.7. Against this backdrop manufacturers saw a renewed
acceleration in cost pressures, with the prices paid index
jumping 6.8pts to 77.9. Firms cited higher steel and aluminium
prices, elevated energy costs, rising freight charges and
ongoing semiconductor shortages, with every major industry
reporting higher input costs during the month.
The past week’s data will have done nothing to bridge the

clear divide across the FOMC, as evident in this week’s busy
Fedspeak. Some participants argued for further rate hikes,
citing strong inflation, diminished supply capacity and policy
that is not yet restrictive. Others counselled patience, pointing
to progress on inflation and that September’s hike buys time
to assess upcoming data. The one thing both agree on is that
inflation will be the primary determinant of the timing and
extent of further rate hikes. Our view remains that one further

hike is likely this cycle to bring policy closer to neutral.
In Australia, this week’s main event was the RBA Monetary
Policy Board’s (MPB) decision to raise the cash rate by 25bps
to 4.60%. This outcome was universally expected given
Governor Bullock had already communicated that upside risks
to inflation had materialised – the conditionality established
by the MPB back in August for considering another rate hike.
Governor Bullock made explicit note of three upside risks.

The Middle East conflict was emphasised as the risk that had
“materialised”, given the re-escalation and renewed surge in crude
oil and refined product prices over recent months. The RBA’s
liaison warns this could lead to further pass-through of costs
and more persistent inflation pressures. On top of this, domestic
capacity pressures and the data centre/AI investment boom were
also noted as upside risks that the MPB remain alert to.
In a video update mid-week, Chief Economist Luci Ellis

explains why the bar for a follow-up rate hike in November is
low. We now expect a 25bp increase in November, provided
there is no sudden resolution of the Middle East conflict
beforehand, or some other event that significantly lowers the
outlook for energy-related costs.
Subsequently, the August CPI revealed that underlying
(trimmed mean) inflation rose 0.2% (3.4%yr), which was
slightly below the market’s expectation but spot on Westpac’s
forecast. As usual, the monthly detail was mixed, but the key

drivers of underlying inflation remain centred on housing-
related components, particularly rents and new dwelling
purchases. Headline inflation remained volatile, with the
unwind of the fuel excise cut causing another spike in fuel
prices. We have maintained our forecast for a 0.9% lift in
trimmed mean inflation in Q3, and with upside risks building
for Q4, a follow-up rate hike in November appears most likely.
Regarding the current growth pulse, household spending

surprised with a flat reading in August. Services spend fell back
following recent strength that was driven by major sporting
events, but this was offset by an increase in goods spend,
particularly in fuel and electric vehicles. Together with our
latest card data for early September, the consumer spending
pulse is shading a touch softer. However, another solid
outcome for business investment could once again be on the
cards for Q3. August’s goods trade data showcased another

significant spike in data centre-related equipment imports, up
$4.3bn, similar in scale to Q1. While clearly import-driven, our
modelling suggests that the local economic impact of such
investment is likely to be around half of the total investment.
Before moving offshore, a final note on housing. Cotality’s
latest update revealed that dwelling prices fell another 1.1%
nationally in September. While Sydney and Melbourne have
Sian Fenner, Head of Business and Industry Economics

Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

### Page 4

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
In China, policymakers announced another round of targeted
support measures aimed at stabilising growth and supporting
the struggling property sector. The PBOC lowered rates on its
Pledged Supplementary Lending facility by 25bps to 1.5% and
expanded quotas for targeted lending programs. Authorities

also introduced subsidies for eligible first-home buyers. The
subsidy applies to homes with a floor area of 120m² or less
and a value below CNY1.5mn, with support capped at 1ppt of
mortgage costs, up to CNY10,000 and for a maximum term of
five years. The measures complement reforms announced in
August to improve developer financing conditions and support
housing completion. However, weak credit demand and
confidence remains a significant constraint, suggesting policy

transmission is still limited.
The September PMI data pointed to a modest improvement
in activity, although the details were less convincing.
Manufacturing returned to expansionary territory, rising 0.9pts
to 50.1, supported by stronger production and new orders.
Much of the improvement, however, reflected a rebound in
prices, with the raw materials price index rising 7.6pts and
producer prices increasing 6.2pts. Labour market conditions
remained weak, with the employment index falling a further

0.6pts and remaining below 50 for almost four years. Conditions
in the non-manufacturing sector also improved, with the
services PMI lifting from 49.0 to 50.2, driven largely by stronger
construction activity. Input and output prices moved higher,
selling prices returned back above 50, export orders improved
to 48.5 and employment increased 0.5pts, although at 45.9 it
remains firmly in contractionary territory. Overall, the surveys
suggest activity is stabilising and policy support is gaining some

traction, but weak labour demand, subdued credit growth and
soft domestic demand indicate the recovery remains fragile.
In Japan, the September Tankan survey delivered another
constructive signal on the economy and reinforced the case
for further policy normalisation. Business conditions improved
by 2pts for large manufacturers and 5pts more broadly, while
profitability indicators strengthened and capital expenditure
intentions remained elevated. Large manufacturers expect

fixed investment to increase by 11.6% in FY2026, while large
non-manufacturers expect investment growth of 11.2%,
pointing to continued confidence in the medium-term outlook.
Capacity indicators improved further across both
manufacturing and non-manufacturing sectors, with measures
of excess production capacity becoming less negative
and indicating that demand remains sufficiently strong to
keep pressure on existing capacity. Labour shortages also

intensified, with employment conditions becoming more
negative across most industries and firm sizes, pointing to a
progressively tighter labour market. Inflation expectations
remained anchored around 2%, providing further evidence
that Japan is moving towards a more sustainable inflation
environment. Overall, the survey was consistent with an
economy operating close to capacity and supports the view
that a December rate hike remains a realistic possibility.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Resilient activity and simmering inflation
While bumpy and uneven, New Zealand’s economic
recovery has continued over the past few months, with
activity showing resilience in the face of continued global
headwinds. Under the surface, however, inflation pressures

are bubbling away, with much of that pressure centred on
parts of the economy that aren’t sensitive to interest rates.
Economic activity getting back on firmer footing
Looking first at recent activity data, the economy has been
finding its feet again after taking a knock in the wake of the
Middle East war. That has been seen most clearly in the jobs
market, with the number of filled jobs rising by 0.9% in the
year to August. While not shooting the lights out, that is still a

noticeable improvement from the early part of the year, when
filled job numbers were falling.
Under the surface, it’s still a very mixed picture. Agriculture
remains the main engine of growth, with the strength in
commodity export earnings helping to boost incomes,
confidence and spending in regions with strong rural backbones.
That’s supported firm job growth in regions like Canterbury
(where filled job numbers are up 1.9% over the past year), Otago

(+1.5%) and Southland (+3.1%). In contrast, jobs growth remains
much more moderate in Auckland (up 0.6%) and Wellington
(up just 0.4%). That town-and-country split is likely to remain a
feature of the economic landscape for some time yet.
Looking ahead, businesses expect trading conditions to
remain resilient through the final months of the year. That
was reflected in September’s Business Outlook survey.
While the survey’s gauges of expected business activity and

general business confidence eased over the past month, they
remain at firm levels, consistent with continued moderate
growth through the latter part of the year. That resilience has
also been evident in the household sector, with consumer
confidence largely steady through September.
The resilience in household and business sentiment is notable,
as fuel prices have taken a big step higher over the past month.
The average price of 91 unleaded has risen by around 49c/litre –

albeit late in the month and likely not fully reflected in surveyed
confidence – taking it back to the levels seen during the early
stages of the Middle East conflict. Those higher fuel prices
have taken a bite out of household disposable incomes, with
spending on fuel up about 22% compared with this time last
year, even though the volume of fuel sold is down around 11%.
But even with the lift in fuel prices, we haven’t seen the same
degree of pessimism as earlier in the year, when fuel prices first

spiked. Notably, those higher fuel prices haven’t spilled over
into a broader lift in output price pressures either (at least at
this stage). Inflation expectations were steady in September’s
household and business surveys. Similarly, the number of firms
planning to raise prices fell back despite a lift in wage and cost
expectations. However, while that stability in pricing gauges is
welcome, measures of inflation pressure remain elevated.
Taken together, recent data reinforce indications that the

economy is shaking off the impacts of the Iran war.
Persistent inflation drivers and the RBNZ
Turning to inflation, the spike in oil prices earlier this year saw
annual inflation climb to 4.1% in the year to June. With local
fuel costs rising again in recent weeks, we continue to expect
inflation to remain above 3% until at least mid-2027.
However, even before the recent rise in fuel costs, inflation had
been running hot for an extended period. Excluding the rise

in food and fuel prices, inflation in the year to June was 2.9%,
and it’s been running close to or above 3% for five years now.
As we discuss in our recent report, much of the strength in
inflation over the past year has been related to administered
costs, such as local council rates, or to sectors that are less
exposed to competitive pressures, such as electricity and
insurance. Those increases have persisted for an extended period,
and we expect further sizeable increases in many of these costs.

This ongoing firmness is a key reason why we expect domestic
(non-tradables) inflation to be higher than the RBNZ expects
over the longer term. It’s also part of the reason why we
expect the RBNZ will ultimately need to raise the OCR by
more than it assumed in its September policy statement.
We are forecasting a 25bp rate rise at the RBNZ’s December
meeting and expect the OCR to peak at 4% next year.
This is a tough environment for the RBNZ. With ongoing

increases in administered prices boosting core inflation at a
time when energy prices are driving headline inflation well
above the target range, it’s hard for the RBNZ to look through
them. The RBNZ had little headroom to absorb upside inflation
surprises, even before the recent spike in oil prices.
Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist

### Page 6

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
But getting inflation back to target in the face of an oil price
spike and high administered prices will not be easy and involves
some tough trade-offs. Administered prices tend to be less
responsive to changes in economic conditions. As a result,
interest rate hikes are likely to have a bigger impact on parts of

the economy that are not the main drivers of inflation. Tighter
policy will also weigh on the labour market and near-term
growth, while increasing households’ debt-servicing costs.
This is an uncomfortable but necessary trade-off. Looking
through, or not responding to, these sorts of large and persistent
cost increases would still leave households facing significant
increases in overall living costs. In the long run, it would be
more damaging for households and the economy if generalised

inflation were to become embedded in price setting behaviour.
The Kiwi has lost its wings
Adding to near-term inflation risks has been the drop in the NZ
dollar. On a trade-weighted basis, the Kiwi has fallen around
4% since the start of September, and it’s begun the December
quarter around 4% below the level the RBNZ assumed in its
September policy statement.
The slide in the Kiwi is mainly due to the strength of the US
dollar, with NZD/USD down nearly 5% since the start of

September to sit just below 0.56 at the time of writing. Moves
against other currencies have been more modest. For example,
NZD/AUD is down 2% over the same period.
Technically, NZD/USD seems oversold. In the short term,
trends in the US dollar will dominate, including any response
to the US labour market report due on Friday evening. Any
turnaround in the USD would likely see NZD/USD trade back
towards the top of the 0.5580–0.5680 range that is governing

the pair for the time being.
NZD/AUD will continue to be driven by changing views on
interest rate differentials between the RBNZ and RBA. This
week, NZD/AUD corrected higher from around 0.8070 to as high
as 0.8110 as RBA cash rate expectations were repriced following
the RBA Board meeting and Australian CPI. In the short term, the
bias for NZD/AUD could be lower, given our view that the RBNZ
will pause at its late-October meeting and that the market is

under-pricing the risk of an RBA rate hike in November.
Next week’s QSBO, due on Tuesday, will be the key event
risk, with the inflation and growth gauges closely watched for
signs that might prompt the RBNZ to pause or hike. Markets
had previously priced in a high chance of a hike at the RBNZ’s
October meeting, but in recent days appear to be reassessing
that view.  Pricing for a 25bp hike in New Zealand could be
pared back towards a 50:50 call. The 0.8030–0.8150 range

is likely to contain the pair in the immediate period ahead.
Our medium-term view is for NZD/AUD to rise as the RBNZ
increases the OCR to 4% by the second half of 2027. We’ll
update our views on the NZD and other currencies in our next
Market Outlook, out Wednesday 14 October.
Fiscal update
The big uncertainty on the domestic horizon is, of course,
November’s general election, and at this stage the polling
looks tight. Neither the established left nor right bloc has a

clear majority.
Ahead of the election, the Treasury has released its Pre-election
Economic and Fiscal Update (PREFU), updating the Budget
2026 (BEFU) fiscal forecasts to reflect the latest economic and
fiscal information and a revised economic outlook. This allows
the public to assess the state of the Government’s finances. It
also provides other parties with a baseline against which to
calibrate their spending and taxation policies.
The PREFU revealed a stronger starting point for the

Government’s finances than had been expected in the Budget.
• A deficit of $8.6bn is estimated for 2025/26, down from the
Budget estimate of $11.9bn.
• Similarly, the forecast deficit for 2026/27 has been revised
down significantly to $6.8bn from $11.4bn.
• Thereafter, the OBEGALx deficit is expected to continue
declining gradually, moving to a surplus of $4bn in 2028/29.
This broadly follows the Budget profile, but is stronger
across the forecast period.

• Net core Crown debt is forecast to peak at 43.9% of GDP in
2027/28, below the 46.1% peak forecast in the BEFU. It’s then
forecast to decline to 39.5% of GDP by the end of the forecast
period in 2030/31, in line with the Government’s goal of
reducing debt below 40% of GDP over the medium term.
Given the improved outlook, NZDM announced a $4bn
reduction in the 2026/27 bond programme to $30bn and a
$15bn reduction in the forecast programme across the whole

period through to 2029/30.
The medium-term forecasts in the PREFU should be treated
with caution. The economic forecasts underpinning the
PREFU were finalised on 21 August, when both oil prices
and, especially, bond yields were materially lower than they
are today. Had the forecasts been finalised more recently, the
fiscal projections presented today would have been weaker,
at least in the near term. Indeed, the Treasury is upfront in
acknowledging that, while the risks are two-sided, the balance

is skewed to the downside.
Aside from the obvious uncertainty surrounding the economic
outlook because of unpredictable developments in the Middle
East, the approaching General Election, and the possibility of
a change in the composition of the Government, means the
assumptions underpinning the PREFU forecasts could quickly
become invalidated.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Oct Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment (index)
Oct 6, Last: 84.4
Consumer sentiment declined 5.2% to 84.4 in September,
taking the index back towards the deeply pessimistic levels seen
earlier in the year. Rising fuel prices and fears of an imminent

interest rate rise drove the move with a continued weakening in
housing markets likely adding to unease in some quarters.
Developments over the last month would have added to these
concerns with: the RBA raising the cash rate by 25bps to 4.6%
during the survey week; average pump prices pushing above
$2.36/litre nationally and no end in sight to global supply
disruptions;  and more weak updates on the housing market.
NZ: Q2 NZIER Survey of Business Opinion (index)

Oct 6, General business confidence last: 12.3
Business conditions picked up modestly in the June quarter as
concerns about the war in the Middle East eased. However,
cost and pricing gauges remained firm. The September quarter
survey will be closely watched ahead of the RBNZ’s 28
October meeting. Recent weeks have seen signs that activity
is firming. However, fuel prices have also risen sharply, and we
(along with the RBNZ) will be watching for signs of a broader

lift in inflation pressures. The timing of survey responses
within the sample period will also warrant scrutiny, given that
oil prices rose late in the quarter.

SUMMARY: AI euphoria meets macro headwinds
Tech remained a major driver of equity market performance in Q3. The
Mag 7 outperformed the broader market while the S&P 500 also

posted gains, lifted by mega-cap and tech stocks.
Still, tech’s path in Q3 was volatile. July began with a reversal of the AI
trade as investors questioned elevated valuations, the sustainability of
AI spending and the outlook for semiconductor demand. That led to a
large sell-off in chip stocks, with large falls in Q2 outperformers SK
Hynix and Samsung contributing to a sharp decline in Korea’s KOSPI.
The Philly SOX experienced its worst monthly performance since

2008.
By August, however, sentiment had improved. The S&P 500 reached
fresh record highs, even as US Treasury yields rose, supported by
stronger cloud earnings and signs of AI monetization. September
proved more challenging as higher oil prices, inflation concerns and
rising bond yields weighed on risk assets, particularly semiconductors.
The Fed also raised its policy rate by 25bp on Sep 16.
Yet, despite these macro headwinds, company guidance continued to

indicate strong AI infrastructure demand, with Micron’s stronger-than-
expected revenue guidance on Sep 30 providing further evidence of
robust demand for memory chips. Memory supply remains tight,
semiconductor capex is accelerating, and the industry’s attention has
increasingly turned to bottlenecks in optical networking and photonics,
as well as concerns around AI regulation and safety. Looking ahead,
tech is likely to remain an important driver of equity returns into Q4,

although elevated valuations and tighter financial conditions leave the
sector sensitive to earnings disappointments. Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, Bloomberg Finance LP. Updated Sep 30. Returns in local currency.
Q3 started with doubts about the AI trade but ended with renewed confidence

## Document 354: Roots and routes – A framework for Global Indian investors

Source PDF: all report pdf/Roots and routes – A framework for Global Indian investors.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Thematic Reports)
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 18
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

WS Global CIO Office

InvesTips

Roots and routes –  
A framework for Global 
Indian investors

September 2026

### Page 2

InvesTips: Roots and routes – A framework for Global Indian investors  |  September 2026  2

India’s financial coming of age has created a generation of investors at a 
distinctive crossroads: deeply connected to one of the world’s fastest -growing 
economies, yet increasingly aware that portfolios concentrated in a single market 
can accumulate risks over time. 
This report discusses the challenges faced by two distinct ‘Global Indian’ investor 
profiles. The first is the onshore Indian investor: a resident Indian whose wealth 
is largely denominated in domestic equities and fixed income, but who may still treat 
global markets as an occasional satellite allocation rather than a core portfolio 
component. The second is the offshore Indian investor : a non -resident Indian 
(NRI) based outside India with full access to global capital markets, yet who may 
be under-allocated to the growth story unfolding in their country of origin. Because 
global benchmarks understate India’s economic importance, investors seeking 
more meaningful participation in its growth story may consider allocating above 
benchmark levels, with NRIs’ potential stronger  affinity with India providing an 
additional reason to make this adjustment. 
For both groups, the starting point is the same: a concentrated bias towards either 
Indian or global markets can create risks that diversification can help mitigate 
without requiring investors to abandon their core convictions. For onshore Indian 
investors, global assets can provide structural currency diversification and access 
to sectors that are under -represented in Indian indices. Our Capital Market 
Assumptions (CMAs)  illustrate the potential portfolio benefits: increasing  
the allocation to global assets from 0% to 15% raises expected returns while 
reducing expected volatility meaningfully. As a result, the portfolio’s risk -adjusted 
returns improve. 
For offshore Indian investors, India offers a real yield premium, a rising weight in global indices and long-term growth that remains 
meaningfully under -represented in global benchmarks. A historical perspective illustrates the opportunity: since 2001,  
Indian equities have delivered over 10% annualised returns in USD terms, outperforming Developed Market equities over the 
same period. 
Onshore and offshore Indian investors may share the same connection to India, but they face very different portfolio challenges. 
There are key dividing lines that determine how each investor should think about diversification, risk management and the rol e 
of Indian assets within a broader investment portfolio.

Manpreet Gill 
Chief Investment Officer, EMEA 
Sylvain Huard 
Head of Asset Allocation 
Hannah Chew 
Portfolio Strategist

1 Two investor profiles, one shared 
opportunity

### Page 3

InvesTips: Roots and routes – A framework for Global Indian investors  |  September 2026  3

Base currency –  
the first and most 
important divide 
 The onshore Indian investor earns, spends, saves and ultimately retires in rupees (INR). Their 
relevant return is, therefore, an INR return. If the INR depreciates against the US dollar (USD), 
foreign assets rise in INR terms and can act as a natural hedge . If the INR appreciates, those 
same assets deliver lower INR returns than their local -currency performance would suggest. 
For the offshore investor, the currency problem is reversed. Their benchmark is typically USD-
based, and Indian markets are the exposure that introduces exchange-rate risk. A strong year 
for Indian equities in INR terms may look less compelling once translated back into USD at an 
unfavourable exchange rate.

Regulatory access –  
different constraints, 
different routes 
 The onshore Indian investor operates within the Liberalised Remittance Scheme (LRS) 
framework. Depending on their circumstances, this can offer meaningful or constrained access 
to global markets. Fund -of-funds and international exchange -traded funds (ETFs)  can also 
offer alternative access routes, as opposed to direct securities alone.  
For the offshore Indian investor, the challenge is less about access to India than choosing the 
most appropriate investment route. They can generally invest through onshore accounts, while 
offshore-listed funds and ETFs may offer a more convenient alternat ive. The preferred 
structure will depend on their residency, tax domicile and product eligibility, making careful 
planning important even where access itself is relatively broad. 
As an example, India’s Gujarat International Finance Tec -City (GIFT City), home to the 
country’s International Financial Services Centre (IFSC), is one potential implementation route 
available to both onshore and offshore Indian investors. For offshore investors, GIFT City offers 
an efficient way to access India-linked and global investment products with little or no need for 
INR conversion, while allowing foreign -currency repatriation. For onshore investors, it can 
facilitate USD -denominated exposure throu gh foreign -currency accounts under the LRS, 
providing a domestically regulated, yet globally oriented platform for portfolio diversification.

Portfolio risk –  
concentration on 
one side, under-
allocation on the 
other 
 The onshore Indian investor’s core risk is concentration and complacency: a portfolio almost 
entirely exposed to India’s domestic economic cycle, policy environment and sectoral 
composition. This is not a prediction that these will deteriorate, as India’s structural growth story 
remains compelling. Rather, this implies the risk is structural when all equity exposure sits 
within a single market, causing the portfolio to react simultaneously to the same domestic 
catalysts. By contrast, the offshore Indian investor faces the opposite problem: under-allocation 
to one of the world’s fastest-growing economies, driven partly by access frictions and partly by 
the difficulty of sizing a home-market allocation through a global investment framework.

### Page 4

InvesTips: Roots and routes – A framework for Global Indian investors  |  September 2026  4

A diversified portfolio makes sense 
regardless of the investor profile 
Before examining the specific assets available to Global 
Indian investors, it is useful to step back and ask a more 
fundamental question: why hold multiple asset classes at all? 
The simple answer is diversification. Because different asset 
classes play dif ferent roles and perform differently across 
economic and market conditions, combining them thoughtfully 
can reduce risk, create more consistent outcomes and build 
greater portfolio resilience than relying on any single asset 
class alone. 
Each major asset class tends to perform best under different 
economic conditions. Equities usually benefit when corporate 
earnings are growing and investor confidence is strong, but 
they can fall sharply when growth disappoints or credit 
conditions tighten. Bonds can provide income and may hold 
their value, or even appreciate, when growth slows and 
central banks ease policy, but they typically face pressure 
when inflation is high and interest rates rise. Gold has long 
been viewed as a geopolitical and infla tion hedge, and its 
relatively low correlation with equities and bonds can make it 
a useful portfolio diversifier. 
In practice, a portfolio concentrated in a single asset class – 
whether Indian equities, USD cash or any other standalone 
exposure – is implicitly relying on one set of economic and 
market conditions remaining favourable. A thoughtfully 
constructed multi-asset portfolio does not need to be the best 
performer in every year, but it is also unlikely to be the worst. 
Its purpose is to build resilience across market cycles: 
capturing growth when conditions are supportive, limiting 
downside when stress emerges, and compounding steadily 
over time in a way that no single asset class can consistently 
achieve on its own.

A practical asset-class toolkit for Global 
Indian investors 
Developed Market (DM) equities 
These are typically shares in large, established companies 
across the US, Europe, the UK and Japan and usually 
represent the largest and most liquid equity markets in the 
world. They are home to the technology giants, global 
healthcare leaders and consumer  brands that dominate their 
sectors globally – many of which have no equivalent in Indian 
indices. DM equities provide the broadest possible access to 
global corporate earnings growth, and for onshore investors, 
they provide USD and other major currency ex posure that 
structurally helps hedge against INR depreciation. For 
offshore investors, DM equities are the natural portfolio core, 
given their base currency and broad exposure of the asset 
class in global markets. 
Emerging Markets (EM) equities  
EM equities span a broad universe of developing economies, 
including China, South Korea, Taiwan, Brazil and other major 
EM markets. They offer greater growth potential than DM 
equities, but also tend to be more volatile. Their returns are 
supported by risi ng consumption, commodity cycles and the 
expansion of industry and the middle class. For Indian 
investors, EM equities provide exposure to regional peers that 
behave differently from Indian domestic markets, adding a 
layer of diversification within the equity sleeve.

1 The building blocks of a Global 
Indian portfolio

### Page 5

InvesTips: Roots and routes – A framework for Global Indian investors  |  September 2026  5

Indian equities  
Indian listed equities can be split up into two distinct sub-asset 
classes: 1) small - and mid -cap equities and 2) large -cap 
equities. The latter represent India’s established blue -chip 
companies with strong balance sheets and have relatively 
lower volatility within the Indian equity spectrum. These are 
typically the anchor of any Indian equity allocation. 
Meanwhile, small - and mid -cap equities tap into a different 
part of India’s growth story: the domestic consumption engine, 
the formalisation of the inform al economy and the 
entrepreneurial mid -market that scales rapidly in a high -
growth environment. They offer meaningfully higher potential 
returns, but also comes with higher volatility and lower 
liquidity, making the appropriate allocation dependent on the 
investor’s risk tolerance and investment time horizon.  
DM Investment Grade bonds 
Developed market investment grade bonds encompass two 
closely related asset classes that play a core defensive role in 
portfolios. Government bonds, such as US Treasuries, 
German Bunds, and UK Gilts, are issued by highly rated 
sovereigns and are widely regarded as the closest equivalent 
to a risk -free asset within global markets. Investment grade 
corporate bonds, issued by financially strong companies rated 
BBB- or above, offer higher yields than government bonds in 
return for accepting a modest amount of credit risk. 
Together, these assets provide an important source of portfolio 
stability. Government bonds have historically performed well 
during periods of economic uncertainty and market turbulence, 
helping to cushion equity losses when investors seek safer 
assets. In vestment grade corporate bonds enhance overall 
income generation while maintaining relatively low volatility, 
allowing investors to improve return potential without taking on 
the higher risks typically associated with equities. 
EM USD bonds 
EM USD bonds are USD -denominated debt issued by EM 
sovereign and quasi-sovereign borrowers, including countries 
such as Indonesia, Mexico, Brazil and South Africa. Their 
USD denomination removes direct exposure to the issuers’ 
local currencies, making them  a more accessible entry point 
into EM fixed income than local -currency bonds, particularly 
for offshore investors with hard -currency liabilities. For 
onshore investors, EM USD bonds add hard -currency fixed-
income exposure and can diversify portfolios conc entrated in 
Indian equities and INR interest -rate risk. However, their 
returns still depend on global interest rates, credit spreads and 
investor risk appetite. 
India government bonds  
The Indian bond market offers something that is increasingly 
rare globally: a genuine positive real (net -of-inflation) yield. 
India’s 10-year government bond yielded around 6.7% in mid-
2026 against a headline inflation of about 4.4%.  This 
represents a positive real yield of nearly 2.8% that compares 
favourably to most DM alternatives. India’s inclusion in global 
bond indices is now also channelling systematic passive 
inflows into this market, providing an additional structural 
demand tailwind.  
Gold 
Gold occupies a unique role in this framework particularly for 
Indian investors. Beyond its universal function as an inflation 
and geopolitical hedge, gold in INR terms has historically 
delivered strong returns during periods of INR weakness 
because gold prices are globally denominated in USD. When 
the INR depreciates, the INR value of gold rises, providing a 
natural cushion against the same currency dynamics that 
erode domestic purchasing power.

The case for global exposure for onshore Indian investors  – Most resident 
Indian investors already diversify across asset classes, typically holding a mix of 
equities, fixed income and, in some cases, commodities such as gold. However, 
this diversification is usually implemented through domestic assets. The gap, 
therefore, is not necessarily a lack of asset-class breadth, but a lack of geographic 
diversification. This is where global investing can play an important role: by adding 
exposure to different economies, currencies, sectors and market cycles  
beyond India.  
Argument

Since India’s market liberalisation in the early 1990s, the INR has depreciated significantly 
against the USD, weakening from approximately INR 31 per USD in 1994 to more than INR 95 
per USD by August 2026 – an annualised depreciation rate of 3.4%. This long-term depreciation 
is not simply a market fluctuation; it reflects a structural feature of an economy that has 
persistently run higher inflation than the US. For investors holding only INR-denominated assets, 
this creates a gradual erosion of global purchasing power over time. USD-denominated foreign 
assets can, therefore, serve as a structural currency hedge, helping preserve international 
purchasing power rather than representing a speculative currency bet. 
The INR 
depreciates 
structurally

1 Why an India-only portfolio may not 
be enough

### Page 7

InvesTips: Roots and routes – A framework for Global Indian investors  |  September 2026  7

Argument

Indian equity indices remain heavily weighted towards the financial, consumer discretionary and 
industrial sectors. By contrast, technology – the largest sector in global DM indices – has a much 
smaller representation in India’s listed market, despite the scale and importance of India’s 
broader technology economy. This gap has become more significant with the rise of artificial 
intelligence (AI). While India has strong technology talent and growing AI adoption, Indian 
markets offer little -to-no exposure to technology in the investible equity market. As a result, 
investors with portfolios concentrated in Indian equities have limited exposure to global 
technology leaders. Adding DM equities, therefore, provides sector diversification, including 
greater partici pation in the growth opportunities associated with AI, as well as geographic 
diversification. 
Sector gaps in 
Indian equity 
markets

### Page 8

InvesTips: Roots and routes – A framework for Global Indian investors  |  September 2026  8

Argument

Indian equities respond sharply to domestic catalysts: RBI rate decisions, monsoon outcomes, 
fiscal borrowing announcements and India -specific political developments. When these factors 
turn negative, they tend to affect domestic equity positions simultane ously. Global equity 
markets are not driven by the same catalysts, so a domestic Indian growth slowdown does not 
cause US corporate earnings to fall or other global equities to retreat. Adding a global equity 
sleeve means that not all equity exposure moves in tandem during a domestic downturn.  
The same logic applies to bonds. Indian bonds respond primarily to RBI monetary policy, 
domestic inflation and India’s fiscal borrowing calendar. These drivers are largely independent 
of the global rate cycle. An investor holding only Indian equities and Indian bonds has all of their 
assets exposed to the same set of domestic macro factors at once. A portfolio that blends Indian 
and global assets reduces this localised cycle dependency, spreading exposure across markets 
that follow different cycles. 
Market cycles are 
not synchronised

Different markets lead at different times because their return cycles are driven by different economic, 
currency and policy forces

InvesTips: Roots and routes – A framework for Global Indian investors  |  September 2026  9

The case for Indian asset exposure for offshore Indian investors  – India 
stands out as one of the few major economies where 6 -7% annual real GDP 
growth remains a credible base case through the end of the decade. For offshore 
investors, the allocation case is not built on growth alone, but on four reinforcing 
pillars: a structural growth premium unmatched among large economies; reforms 
that have improved the quality and breadth of that growth; a demographic 
dividend that still has years to run; and a digital public infrastructure revolution 
that is lifting productivity at population scale.

The growth premium  
According to the International Monetary Fund, India remains the world’s fastest-growing major economy. Real 
GDP expanded by 7.6% in 2025 and is projected to grow by 6.5% in 2026 – outpacing China at 4.4%, G7 
economies at 1.6%, and EM and developing economies at 3.9%. This is not a one-year outlier. Apart from the 
Covid-19 pandemic shock, India has sustained a 6 -8% real GDP growth range for much of the past decade, 
reinforcing its structural growth premium. 
This structural growth is also reflected in equity -market performance. Since 2001, Indian equities have 
delivered annualised returns of 10 .1% in USD terms , outperforming DM equities, which returned 7.5% 
annualised over the same period. This long-term track record reinforces the case for including Indian equities 
as a deliberate source of growth within an offshore investor’s portfolio. 
For offshore investors, there is a clear benchmark gap. A global equity benchmark allocation of around 1.3% 
to India materially understates the country’s economic weight. India accounts for roughly 8.2% of global GDP 
on a purchasing power parity basis in 2025, which is significantly higher than its equity index weight. As Indian 
capital markets deepen and foreign ownership constraints ease, this gap between economic weight and index 
representation should continue to narrow, while rising benchmark inclusion can create a recurring source of 
passive inflows into Indian assets.

Economic and market reforms 
India's growth story over the past decade has not just been about the pace of GDP expansion. It also reflects 
a series of market -oriented reforms that have strengthened the investment environment and made growth 
more durable and accessible to long -term cap ital. Examples include the Goods and Services Tax, the 
Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code, the Real Estate Regulation and Development Act and measures to deepen 
and formalise domestic capital markets. Taken together, these reforms have improved the structural 
competitiveness of the Indian economy in ways that show up in productivity growth, foreign direct investment 
inflows and market depth – not just in headline GDP numbers.

1 Why Indian assets still belong in every 
offshore Indian investor portfolio

### Page 10

InvesTips: Roots and routes – A framework for Global Indian investors  |  September 2026  10

Demographic dividend  
India’s demographic advantage is a long -term structural tailwind, not a short -term population bulge. The 
country has the world’s largest working -age population and, with a median age of around 29.2 (according to 
Worldometer’s latest data), remains one of the youngest major economies. As more Indians enter the formal 
workforce, earn higher incomes and build financial savings, they can support a broader domestic consumption 
base, deeper financial intermediation and continued cap ital-market growth. The shift in household savings 
from physical assets, such as gold and real estate, towards financial assets reinforces this trend. Monthly 
systematic investment plan (SIP) inflows into mutual funds, which have risen from approximately INR 36.6bn 
in 2016 to more than IN R 310bn in 2026, are a clear expression of this demographic and savings transition. 
They also create a durable, domestically -sourced demand base for Indian equities that is less dependent on 
foreign capital flows. 
However, the demographic dividend is not automatic; its realisation will depend on equipping a growing 
workforce with employable, industry -relevant skills and creating sufficient productive jobs. India has made 
meaningful investments in this direction thro ugh the Skill India Mission, including vocational training, 
apprenticeships and courses aligned with emerging sectors. Sustained improvements in training quality, 
employer alignment and job creation will, therefore, be critical to convert India’s demographic scale into lasting 
gains in productivity, income and economic growth.

Digital infrastructure 
India's digital public infrastructure – collectively known as the India Stack – has built a low-cost, inter-operable 
rails system that is accelerating formalisation, financial inclusion and commercial productivity at a speed with 
few global parallels. Exam ples include Aadhaar for identity, the Unified Payments Interface for payments, 
DigiLocker for documentation and the Open Network for Digital Commerce. Together, these platforms are 
productivity infrastructure: reducing the cost of doing business, formalising the informal economy and 
expanding the addressable market for financial services companies, fintechs, consumer platforms and logistics 
businesses in ways that compound into earnings growth over time.

The India fixed income opportunity 
India's 10-year government bond yielded around 6.7% in mid -2026, against headline consumer inflation of 
approximately 4.4% – implying a real yield of around 2.4%. This compares to near-zero or negative real yields 
in much of the developed world over the past decade and to real yields of 1-2% available in most comparable 
EM bond markets.

For onshore Indian investors, adding global assets can improve portfolio 
resilience without diluting expected INR returns. Our modelling shows that moving 
from a fully domestic portfolio to a 15% global allocation lifts the expected return 
only marginally,  from 8.42% to 8.44%, but reduces expected volatility more 
meaningfully, from 18.38% to 16.86%. This improves the risk-adjusted return from 
0.46 to 0.50. The benefit comes from exposure to markets, sectors and currencies 
that do not move in lockstep with I ndian assets, helping reduce domestic 
concentration risk while adding access to different structural growth drivers.

### Page 12

InvesTips: Roots and routes – A framework for Global Indian investors  |  September 2026  12

For offshore investors with globally diversified portfolios, Indian assets are best viewed as a deliberate 
strategic tilt towards a differentiated source of long -term growth, rather than as a near -term portfolio 
efficiency trade. Based on our forward -looking Capital Market Assumptions (CMAs), increasing the 
allocation to Indian assets to 15% leaves the expected USD return broadly unchanged at 6.10%, while 
expected volatility rises from 10.32% to 11.00%, reflecting the additional EM and currency risks.  
However, these aggregate estimates may not fully capture the opportunity in Indian financial market assets. 
Our CMAs show that the case for Indian equities is compelling when viewed from a returns perspective 
alone. 
The challenge to portfolio efficiency arises from Indian equities’ relatively high expected volatility. For 
context, our CMAs suggest an expected volatility of 29% for Indian large -cap equities. This number is 
considerably higher than the level experienced over the last 10 years (18.9%) and modestly higher than the 
level over the past several decades. This is a key point because the drag on risk-adjusted returns in Fig. 8 
comes from the higher expected volatility of Indian equities rather than any challenge to the expected return. 
As an example, Fig. 9 illustrates that calibrating Indian equity volatility to more recent market experience 
produces a more balanced assessment of portfolio risk and return. Using recent history as an example 
shows that risk-adjusted returns actually improve modestly from 0.79 to 0.81.  
Overall, this means the debate is less about how high a return Indian equities are expected to deliver over 
the coming years. Instead, how volatile Indian assets are likely to be is key to whether adding them to global 
portfolios improves risk-adjusted returns.

### Page 13

InvesTips: Roots and routes – A framework for Global Indian investors  |  September 2026 13
Pulling this together, we would argue that a strong case exists for a measured allocation to Indian assets –
particularly equities – exists, in our view, provided two conditions are met.
First, investors should retain conviction that the structural drivers of India’s return premium – including
earnings growth, improving governance and an expanding investable universe – remain intact. This is key

to being comfortable with the expected return of Indian equities and bonds.
Second, investors must be willing and able to tolerate the volatility associated with Indian equities. While
one could argue that the assumed volatility in our CMAs is high relative to the level experienced in recent
decades, under most scenarios, it would remain meaningfully higher than that of DM equities.
Taken together, offshore Indian investors should consider incorporating Indian assets within a globally

diversified portfolio. However, the allocation should be deliberate and sized in line with their objectives and
risk tolerance, reflecting a conscious investment view and not just the familiarity of a home bias.

CMAs.
These results show that cross-border diversification serves different needs for different investors but leads
to the same portfolio conclusion. For onshore Indian investors, global assets can help lower volatility,

broaden sector exposure and provide a structural hedge against INR depreciation. For offshore Indian
investors, India offers a deliberate growth and real -yield allocation that is under -represented in global
benchmarks. In both cases, combining Indian and global assets can build a more resil ient portfolio across
market cycles.

### Page 14

InvesTips: Roots and routes – A framework for Global Indian investors  |  September 2026  14

Resident onshore Indian investors and offshore Indian investors begin from opposite starting points. One 
is anchored in India and looking outward; the other is anchored globally and looking inward. Their regulatory 
regimes, base currencies and available instruments differ. Yet the portfolio destination is the same: 
meaningful exposure to both India and the world. 
That conclusion reflects the reality of an interconnected investment world. Indian corporate earnings are 
shaped by global commodity prices, technology supply chains and demand from DMs. At the same time, 
companies in the US, Europe and Japan are exposed to the consumption growth of emerging economies, 
including India. Bond markets may respond first to domestic central banks, but they are still influenced by 
global rate cycles; gold is priced in USD, yet its impact is felt across currencies. No investor can  afford to 
treat the other half of the world as irrelevant. 
What differs is not the principle, but the path. The onshore Indian investor adds global exposure as a 
deliberate extension of an India -centric portfolio, seeking additional return opportunities while gaining a 
structural hedge against INR depreciation. Th e offshore investor builds India exposure as a deliberate 
overweight above global benchmarks, accepting INR volatility as the cost of accessing India’s growth 
premium and real-yield opportunity. The appropriate allocation will differ by profile and risk appetite, but the 
core message is the same: both investors need both markets.

崇越 5434 TT  ( 電子–電子通路 ) 
先進製程擴張為營運成長基礎 
投資與評價 
1. 光阻劑、矽晶圓、光罩等半導體核心材料需求持續強勁，另環保工程業
務受惠於大型專案認列，2Q26 營收優於我們先前預期。 
2. 綜觀 7~9 月營收皆維持高檔，研究部推估主要受惠於晶圓代工業產能
持續擴張，且另有漲價挹注營收表現，3Q26 營收為 236.7 億元。 
3. 半導體主力產品及新品需求皆見成長，帶動半導體材料業務持續維持季
增，研究部上調 FY26 光阻劑營收全年年增率達 30%以上。 
4. 研究部上調 FY26/FY27 營收獲利預估，預估 FY26/FY27 EPS 分別為 
30.72/32.34 元。研究部維持買進投資建議，目標價調升至 700 元，以
21x FY27 獲利預估。 
重點更新 
半導體材料業務仍為今明年成長主旋律 
半導體主力產品及新品需求皆見成長，帶動半導體材料業務持續維持季增。
研究部上調 FY26 光阻劑營收全年年增率達 30%以上。矽晶圓市場需求情況
逐漸轉好 。 藍寶石基板 、 散熱材料等新品亦開始於1H26 成長力道逐漸顯著 ，
使其他電子材料 1H26 年增率達 30%以上。研究部預期產品調漲效應將延續
至 4Q26， 帶動公司FY26 整體營運逐季走高 ，FY27 營運表現亦將優於 FY26。 
工程業務 2H26 進入工程傳統認列旺季 
大型案件有所斬獲，環保工程在手訂單約近 200 億水平。隨下半年進入工
程傳統認列旺季，研究部預期營收將逐季走高，預估 FY26 全年工程業務營
收將年增 20%以上。崇越持續進行海外布局，目前東南亞案件持續進行
中，亦逐漸朝美國 AI 領域工程拓展，今年將陸續於多國設立據點，奠定工
程業務及半導體材料業務海外發展基礎。 
先進製程擴張為營運成長基礎 
信越於先進製程穩固的市佔率奠定崇越營運成長基礎。受惠先進製程產能持
續釋出且 UTR 維持高檔，加上成熟製程於 2H26 復甦趨勢確立，晶圓代工
產業動能明確。在此背景下，崇越憑藉於光阻劑與矽晶圓的穩固市占，並積
極布局先進封裝材料，奠定產業龍頭地位；此外，工程業務營收認列持續改
善，疊加在手訂單持續維持高水平，挹注營運動能。考量半導體材料業務動
能提升，研究部上調 FY26/FY27 營收獲利預估。

### Page 2

訪談內容 
半導體核心材料需求強勁、環工業務受惠於大型專案認列挹注 2Q26 營收表現 
光阻劑、矽晶圓、光罩等半導體核心材料需求持續強勁，另環保工程業務受惠於大型專案認列，
營收呈現季增，使 2Q26 營收達 208.3 億元(YoY+22.6%/QoQ +12.3%)，創下歷史新高，亦優
於 我 們 先 前 預 期 的 194.1 億元。 產 品 組 合 轉 好帶動 毛利率 持 續 維 持 高 檔 ， 為13.5% 
(YoY+1.8ppts/ QoQ-0.1ppts)，符合我們先前預期的 13.3%。EPS 創下歷史新高，達 7.92 元
(YoY+65.5%/QoQ+15.8%)，優於研究部原先預期的 6.58 元。 
綜觀 7~9 月營收皆維持高檔，研究部推估主要受惠於晶圓代工業產能持續擴張，且另有漲價挹
注營收表現， 3Q26 營收為 236.7 億元 (YoY+34.9%/QoQ+13.6%)；預估毛利率將 持穩於
13.3%(QoQ -0.2ppts/ YoY +1.6ppts) ， 產 品 組 合 維 持 穩 定；預期 EPS 為 8.42 元
(YoY+46.7%/QoQ+6.4%)。
